.

6
Proof of Theorem
4.2

In a first easy step, we will show that
\bm{p}^{\textnormal{VOL}}
and
\bm{q}^{\textnormal{VOL}}
both belong to
{\mathcal{A}_{\exp}}
. Recall the definition of the kernels
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
(
4.2
) and the assumption (
4.3
) on them. We can now readily exponentially dominate them:

\displaystyle\bm{p}^{\textnormal{VOL}}

\displaystyle=a_{1}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}\int_{[0,\infty)}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{1}(\mathrm{d}x)+a_{2}\left({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}-\frac{1}{2}K_{2}(0)b_{2}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}\right)\int_{[0,\infty)}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{2}(\mathrm{d}x)+y{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}

(6.1)

\displaystyle\preceq\left(\left|a_{1}\right|+\left|a_{2}\right|+\frac{1}{2}\left|a_{2}b_{2}K_{2}(0)\right|\right)({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}+{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}})\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}^{\otimes n}\int_{[0,\infty)}x^{n}(\mu_{1}(\mathrm{d}x)+\mu_{2}(\mathrm{d}x))

(6.2)

\displaystyle\preceq\left(\left|a_{1}\right|+\left|a_{2}\right|+\frac{1}{2}\left|a_{2}b_{2}K_{2}(0)\right|\right)({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}+{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}})e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}M{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}+\left|y\right|{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}},

(6.3)

\displaystyle\bm{q}^{\textnormal{VOL}}

\displaystyle=b_{1}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}\int_{[0,\infty)}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{1}({\mathrm{d}x})+b_{2}\left({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}-\frac{1}{2}K_{2}(0)b_{2}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}\right)\int_{[0,\infty)}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{2}(\mathrm{d}x)

(6.4)

\displaystyle\preceq\left(\left|b_{1}\right|+\left|b_{2}\right|+\frac{1}{2}\left|b_{2}^{2}K_{2}(0)\right|\right)({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}+{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}})e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}M{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}.

(6.5)

By Corollary
3.13
,
\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}
belongs to
{\mathcal{A}_{\exp}}
and in particular to
\mathcal{I}(\widehat{W})
. The process

\displaystyle\bar{Y}_{t}:=\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle

(6.6)

is then well-defined and admits the Itô decomposition

\displaystyle\bar{Y}_{t}

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}\right)^{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{22}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}W_{s}.

(6.7)

It then remains to prove that
\bar{Y}
solves the equation

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{d}\bar{Y}_{t}&=K_{1}(0)\left(a_{1}+b_{1}\bar{Y}_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{t}K_{1}^{\prime}(t-s)\left(a_{1}+b_{1}\bar{Y}_{s}\right)\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}t\\
&\quad+K_{2}(0)\left(a_{2}+b_{2}\bar{Y}_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\int_{0}^{t}K_{2}^{\prime}(t-s)\left(a_{2}+b_{2}\bar{Y}_{s}\right)\mathrm{d}W_{s}\mathrm{d}t,\\
\bar{Y}_{0}&=y,\end{split}

(6.8)

which is the differential version of (
4.1
) obtained from Itô’s lemma, since the kernels
K_{1},K_{2}
are smooth. Then, we immediately deduce that
Y_{t}=\bar{Y}_{t}=\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle
by uniqueness of the strong solution, which will complete the proof of Theorem
4.2
.

Lemma 6.1
.

We have that

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{22}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=K_{1}(0)\left(a_{1}+b_{1}\bar{Y}_{t}\right)

(6.9)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}K_{1}^{\prime}(t-s)\left(a_{1}+b_{1}\bar{Y}_{s}\right)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}K_{2}^{\prime}(t-s)\left(a_{2}+b_{2}\bar{Y}_{s}\right)\mathrm{d}W_{s},

(6.10)

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=K_{2}(0)(a_{2}+b_{2}\bar{Y}_{t}).

(6.11)

6.1
Proof of Lemma
6.1

Now, as we will be using integral of elements of
T((\mathbb{R}^{2}))
, recall the Definition
4.1
, we need to make sure its bracket with the signature and concatenation make sense. This is the goal of the next two lemmas:

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
\bm{p}\in L^{1}(T((\mathbb{R}^{2})),\mu)
, then
\bm{\ell}\bm{p},\bm{p}\bm{\ell}\in L^{1}(T((\mathbb{R}^{2})),\mu)
for every
\bm{\ell}\in T((\mathbb{R}^{2}))
,

\int_{[0,\infty)}\bm{\ell}\bm{p}(x)\mu(\mathrm{d}x)=\bm{\ell}\left(\int_{[0,\infty)}\bm{p}(x)\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\right)

and

\int_{[0,\infty)}\bm{p}(x)\bm{\ell}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)=\left(\int_{[0,\infty)}\bm{p}(x)\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\right)\bm{\ell}.

Proof.

Obvious.
∎

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
\bm{p}\in L^{1}(T((\mathbb{R}^{2})),\mu)
such that
\int_{[0,\infty)}\left|\left|\bm{p}(x)\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)<\infty
for every
t\in[0,T]
, then

\left\langle\int_{[0,\infty)}\bm{p}(x)\mu(\mathrm{d}x),\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle=\int_{[0,\infty)}\left\langle\bm{p}(x),\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\mu(\mathrm{d}x).

Proof.

By assumption

\displaystyle\int_{[0,\infty)}\left|\left|\bm{p}(x)\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,\infty)}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left|\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V_{n}}\bm{p}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}(x)\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\right|\mu(\mathrm{d}x)<\infty.

(6.12)

This implies that
\bm{p}(x)\in\mathcal{A}

\mu
-a.e., so by the dominated convergence theorem

\displaystyle\left\langle\int_{[0,\infty)}\bm{p}(x)\mu(\mathrm{d}x),\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V_{n}}\left(\int_{[0,\infty)}\bm{p}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}(x)\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\right)\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,\infty)}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V_{n}}\bm{p}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}(x)\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,\infty)}\left\langle\bm{p}(x),\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\mu(\mathrm{d}x).

∎

We now introduce two important propositions, which establish the commutativity of the kernel integral and the bracket.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
\bm{\ell}\in{\mathcal{A}_{h}}
. Assume that
\mu
is a measure on
[0,\infty)
satisfying
\int_{[0,\infty)}x^{n}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)<M^{n}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and a positive constant
M
. We then have for
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}={{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{\ell}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\left(\int_{[0,\infty)}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\right),\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\int_{[0,\infty)}\bm{\ell}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x),\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle

(6.13)

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,\infty)}\left\langle\bm{\ell}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\mu(\mathrm{d}x).

(6.14)

Proof.

We start by proving the first equality (
6.13
). Fix
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\in\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}\right\}
. By assumption of
\mu
, it is easy to show that
\int_{[0,\infty)}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\in{\mathcal{A}_{h}}
by direct computation. In particular
xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\in L^{1}(\mathcal{A},\mu)
. A direct application of Lemma
6.2
shows that

\bm{\ell}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\left(\int_{[0,\infty)}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\right)=\int_{[0,\infty)}\bm{\ell}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\in T((\mathbb{R}^{2})).

Moreover, Proposition
3.7
ensures that it actually belongs to
{\mathcal{A}_{h}}
so that the bracket is well-defined, which proves the first equality.

For the second equality (
6.14
), it suffices to verify that
\int_{[0,\infty)}\left|\left|\bm{\ell}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)<\infty
for every
t\in[0,T]
:

\displaystyle\int_{[0,\infty)}\left|\left|\bm{\ell}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{[0,\infty)}\left|\left|\bm{\ell}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\leq\int_{[0,\infty)}\left|\left|\bm{\ell}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}}\left|\left|{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}}\left|\left|xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x),

where the second equality comes from Proposition
3.7
. It then follows

\displaystyle\int_{[0,\infty)}\left|\left|\bm{\ell}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\bm{\ell}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}}\left|\left|{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}}\int_{[0,\infty)}\left|\left|xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|\bm{\ell}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}}\left|\left|{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}}\int_{[0,\infty)}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}x^{n+1}(n+1)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\frac{(2t)^{n}}{\sqrt{(2n-1)!}}\right)\mu(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|\bm{\ell}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}}\left|\left|{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}M^{n+1}(n+1)^{-\frac{1}{4}}\frac{(2t)^{n}}{\sqrt{(2n-1)!}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|\bm{\ell}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}}\left|\left|{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}}M\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}M^{n}\frac{(2t)^{n}}{(n-1)!}+1\right)

\displaystyle=C\left|\left|\bm{\ell}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}}\left|\left|{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}}M\left(2tMe^{2tM}+1\right)

\displaystyle<\infty.

An application of Lemma
6.3
completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 6.5
.

\mu
is a measure on
[0,\infty)
satisfying
\int_{[0,\infty)}x^{n}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)<M^{n}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and a positive constant
M
, then

\displaystyle\int_{[0,\infty)}\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\mu(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}J^{\prime}(t-s)\bar{Y}_{s}\mathrm{d}s

(6.15)

and

\displaystyle\int_{[0,\infty)}\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\mu(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}J^{\prime}(t-s)\bar{Y}_{s}\mathrm{d}W_{s}-\frac{1}{2}K_{2}(0)\int_{0}^{t}J^{\prime}(t-s)(a_{2}+b_{2}\bar{Y}_{s})\mathrm{d}s

(6.16)

where
J(t)=\int_{[0,\infty)]}e^{-xt}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
A.1
and the shuffle property, we derive

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=x\left\langle e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}s=x\int_{0}^{t}e^{-x(t-s)}\bar{Y}_{s}\mathrm{d}s,

(6.17)

Similarly, using Lemma
A.1
and the shuffle property,

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=x\left\langle e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\left\langle\left(e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle

(6.18)

\displaystyle=x\left\langle e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}W_{s}

(6.19)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}x\left\langle e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}s

(6.20)

\displaystyle=x\int_{0}^{t}e^{-x(t-s)}\bar{Y}_{s}\mathrm{d}W_{s}+\frac{1}{2}K_{2}(0)x\int_{0}^{t}e^{-x(t-s)}(a_{2}+b_{2}\bar{Y}_{t})\mathrm{d}s,

(6.21)

where the second and third equalities come from Theorem
3.2
and (
6.29
) respectively.

We will now show that we can apply the deterministic and stochastic Fubini theorem. First, noticing that for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\mathbb{E}\left|\bar{Y}_{t}\right|=\mathbb{E}\left|\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\right|\leq\left|\left|\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}},

and thus

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left|\bar{Y}_{t}\right|<\infty.

We can now apply Tonelli’s theorem:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{[0,\infty)}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-x(t-s)}\left|\bar{Y}_{s}\right|\mathrm{d}s~\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,\infty)}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-x(t-s)}\mathbb{E}\left|\bar{Y}_{s}\right|\mathrm{d}s~\mu(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{[0,\infty)}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left|\bar{Y}_{s}\right|\mathrm{d}s~\mu(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\leq\mu([0,\infty))\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left|\bar{Y}_{s}\right|\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\mu([0,\infty))~T\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left|\bar{Y}_{t}\right|<\infty.

This leads to

\int_{[0,\infty)}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-x(t-s)}\left|\bar{Y}_{s}\right|\mathrm{d}s~\mu(\mathrm{d}x)<\infty\textnormal{ a.s.}.

(6.22)

Now we can apply Fubini theorem on equation (
6.17
) and derive:

\int_{[0,\infty)}\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\mu(\mathrm{d}x)=\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{[0,\infty)}xe^{-x(t-s)}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\right)\bar{Y}_{s}\mathrm{d}s=-\int_{0}^{t}J^{\prime}(t-s)\bar{Y}_{s}\mathrm{d}s,

thus proving (
6.15
). On the other hand, by Proposition
3.6

(ii)
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left[\bar{Y}_{t}^{2}\right]\leq\left(\left|\left|\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}\right|\right|_{T}^{\mathcal{A}_{h}}\right)^{2}<\infty

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{[0,\infty)}\sqrt{\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2x(t-s)}\left|\bar{Y}_{s}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}s}~\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mu([0,\infty))\mathbb{E}\left[\sqrt{\int_{0}^{t}\left|\bar{Y}_{s}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}s}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mu([0,\infty))\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\left|\bar{Y}_{s}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\mu([0,\infty))\sqrt{T\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\left|\bar{Y}_{t}\right|^{2}}<\infty,

which leads to

\int_{[0,\infty)}\sqrt{\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2x(t-s)}\left|\bar{Y}_{s}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}s}~\mu(\mathrm{d}x)<\infty\textnormal{ a.s.}

(6.23)

Note that (
6.23
) is actually the condition for stochastic Fubini theorem to hold, see
[
45
, Theorem 2.2]
. Applying it, we derive

\displaystyle\int_{[0,\infty)}\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\mu(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}J^{\prime}(t-s)\bar{Y}_{s}\mathrm{d}W_{s}-\frac{1}{2}K_{2}(0)\int_{0}^{t}J^{\prime}(t-s)(a_{2}+b_{2}\bar{Y}_{s})\mathrm{d}s,

thus completing the proof.
∎

Now, taking
\mu=\mu_{1},\mu_{2}
, we combine Proposition
6.4
with Proposition
6.5
and derive, for
i=1,2
:

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}\int_{[0,\infty)}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{i}(\mathrm{d}x),\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}K_{i}^{\prime}(t-s)\bar{Y}_{s}\mathrm{d}s,

(6.24)

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}\int_{[0,\infty)}xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{i}(\mathrm{d}x),\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{t}K_{i}^{\prime}(t-s)\bar{Y}_{s}\mathrm{d}W_{s}-\frac{1}{2}K_{2}(0)\int_{0}^{t}K_{i}^{\prime}(t-s)(a_{2}+b_{2}\bar{Y}_{s})\mathrm{d}s.\quad

(6.25)

Finally, we can now compute the projections
\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}},\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{22}}}}
. First recall

\displaystyle\bm{p}^{\textnormal{VOL}}

\displaystyle=a_{1}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}\int_{[0,\infty)}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{1}(\mathrm{d}x)+a_{2}\left({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}-\frac{1}{2}K_{2}(0)b_{2}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}\right)\int_{[0,\infty)}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{2}(\mathrm{d}x)+y{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}},

(6.26)

\displaystyle\bm{q}^{\textnormal{VOL}}

\displaystyle=b_{1}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}\int_{[0,\infty)}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{1}({\mathrm{d}x})+b_{2}\left({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}-\frac{1}{2}K_{2}(0)b_{2}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}\right)\int_{[0,\infty)}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{2}(\mathrm{d}x).

(6.27)

We then have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bm{p}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}&=a_{1}\int_{[0,\infty)}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{1}(\mathrm{d}x)-a_{2}\int_{[0,\infty)}\left(x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}+\frac{1}{2}K_{2}(0)b_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}\right)e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{2}(\mathrm{d}x),\\
\bm{q}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}&=b_{1}\int_{[0,\infty)}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{1}(\mathrm{d}x)-b_{2}\int_{[0,\infty)}\left(x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}+\frac{1}{2}K_{2}(0)b_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}\right)e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{2}(\mathrm{d}x),\\
\bm{p}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}}&=\int_{[0,\infty)}\mu_{2}(dx)a_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}=K_{2}(0)a_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}},\qquad\bm{p}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{22}}}}=0,\\
\bm{q}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}}&=\int_{[0,\infty)}\mu_{2}(dx)b_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}=K_{2}(0)b_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}},\qquad\bm{q}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{22}}}}=0.\end{split}

(6.28)

Then, using Lemma
A.2
and noticing
(\bm{q}^{\textnormal{VOL}})^{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}=0
, it immediately follows that

\displaystyle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}}

\displaystyle=\bm{p}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}}+\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}\left(\bm{q}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}}\right)=K_{2}(0)(a_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+b_{2}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}),

(6.29)

\displaystyle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{22}}}}

\displaystyle=K_{2}(0)b_{2}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}}=K_{2}^{2}(0)b_{2}(a_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+b_{2}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}),

(6.30)

and that

\displaystyle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}

\displaystyle=\bm{p}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}+\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}\left(\bm{q}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\right)

(6.31)

\displaystyle=\left(a_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+b_{1}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}\right)\int_{[0,\infty)}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{1}(\mathrm{d}x)-\left(a_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+b_{2}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}\right)\int_{[0,\infty)}\left(x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}+\frac{1}{2}K_{2}(0)b_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}\right)e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{2}(\mathrm{d}x)

(6.32)

\displaystyle=K_{1}(0)\left(a_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+b_{1}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}K_{2}^{2}(0)b_{2}\left(a_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+b_{2}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}\right)

(6.33)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(a_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+b_{1}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}\int_{[0,\infty)}-xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{1}(\mathrm{d}x)

(6.34)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(a_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+b_{2}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}\right)\left({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}-\frac{1}{2}K_{2}(0)b_{2}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}\right)\int_{[0,\infty)}-xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{2}(\mathrm{d}x).

(6.35)

Proof of Lemma
6.1
.

Following directly from (
6.29
)-(
6.30
), we get

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=K_{2}(0)(a_{2}+b_{2}\bar{Y}_{t})\quad\text{and}\quad\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{22}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle=K_{2}^{2}(0)b_{2}(a_{2}+b_{2}\bar{Y}_{t}).

(6.36)

It now only remains to compute
\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle
. For this, recall (
6.33
)

\displaystyle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}

\displaystyle=K_{1}(0)\left(a_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+b_{1}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}K_{2}^{2}(0)b_{2}\left(a_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+b_{2}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}\right)

(6.37)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(a_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+b_{1}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}\int_{[0,\infty)}-xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{1}(\mathrm{d}x)

(6.38)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(a_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+b_{2}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}\right)\left({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}-\frac{1}{2}K_{2}(0)b_{2}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}\right)\int_{[0,\infty)}-xe^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-x{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\mu_{2}(\mathrm{d}x).

(6.39)

A direct application of equation (
6.24
) and (
6.25
) yields:

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{VOL}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=K_{1}(0)\left(a_{1}+b_{1}\bar{Y}_{t}\right)-\frac{1}{2}K_{2}^{2}(0)b_{2}(a_{2}+b_{2}\bar{Y}_{t})

(6.40)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}K_{1}^{\prime}(t-s)\left(a_{1}+b_{1}\bar{Y}_{s}\right)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}K_{2}^{\prime}(t-s)\left(a_{2}+b_{2}\bar{Y}_{s}\right)\mathrm{d}W_{s},

(6.41)

which concludes the proof of Lemma
6.1
and thus of Theorem
4.2
.
∎

7
Proof of Theorem
4.4

It is straightforward to see that
\bm{p}^{\textnormal{DE}}\in{\mathcal{A}_{\exp}}
as

\bm{p}^{\textnormal{DE}}=\left(a_{1}-\tfrac{1}{2}b_{2}a_{2}\right){{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}+a_{2}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}+z{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}.

Using Proposition
3.12

(iv)
-
(i)
we can easily see
\bm{q}^{\textnormal{DE}}\in{\mathcal{A}_{\exp}}
as

\bm{q}^{\textnormal{DE}}=\left(\left(b_{1}-\tfrac{1}{2}b_{2}^{2}\right){\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+\sum_{m=1}^{k_{1}}c_{1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\alpha_{1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}-\tfrac{1}{2}b_{2}\sum_{m=1}^{k_{2}}c_{2}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\alpha_{2}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}+\left(b_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+\sum_{m=1}^{k_{2}}c_{2}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\alpha_{2}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\right){{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}.

Then applying Corollary
3.13
we have
\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}\in{\mathcal{A}_{\exp}}
and in particular
\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}\in\mathcal{I}(\widehat{W})
.

Now recalling Lemma
A.2
, i.e. for all
\bm{p},\bm{q}\in T((\mathbb{R}^{d}))
such that
\bm{q}^{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}=0
,

\displaystyle\left(\bm{p}{\left({\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}-\bm{q}\right)}^{-1}\right)|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}}

\displaystyle=\bm{p}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}}+\bm{p}{\left({\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}-\bm{q}\right)}^{-1}(\bm{q}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}}),

(7.1)

it directly follows that

\displaystyle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}

\displaystyle=\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}\left(\left(b_{1}-\tfrac{1}{2}b_{2}^{2}\right){\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+\sum_{m=1}^{k_{1}}c_{1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\alpha_{1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}-\tfrac{1}{2}b_{2}\sum_{m=1}^{k_{2}}c_{2}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\alpha_{2}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\right)+\left(a_{1}-\tfrac{1}{2}b_{2}a_{2}\right){\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}},

(7.2)

\displaystyle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}}

\displaystyle=\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}\left(b_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+\sum_{m=1}^{k_{2}}c_{2}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\alpha_{2}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\right)+a_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}},

(7.3)

\displaystyle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{22}}}}

\displaystyle=b_{2}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}}.

(7.4)

Finally,

\displaystyle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{22}}}}

\displaystyle=\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}\left(b_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+\sum_{m=1}^{k_{1}}c_{1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\alpha_{1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\right)+a_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}.

(7.5)

Now ready to apply Theorem
3.3
on
\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}
, we get

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}\right)^{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{22}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}W_{s}

(7.6)

\displaystyle=z+\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}\left(b_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+\sum_{m=1}^{k_{1}}c_{1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\alpha_{1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\right)+a_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}s

(7.7)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}\left(b_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+\sum_{m=1}^{k_{2}}c_{2}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\alpha_{2}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\right)+a_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}W_{s}

(7.8)

\displaystyle=z+\int_{0}^{t}\left(a_{1}+b_{1}\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle+\sum_{m=1}^{k_{1}}c_{1}^{m}\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\alpha_{1}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle\right)\mathrm{d}s

(7.9)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}\left(a_{2}+b_{2}\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle+\sum_{m=1}^{k_{2}}c_{2}^{m}\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\alpha_{2}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle\right)\mathrm{d}W_{s}.

(7.10)

Finally, using Lemma
A.1
, i.e.
\bm{\ell}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\gamma{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}}=e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\gamma{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\left(\left(e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-\gamma{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\ell}\right){{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\right)
for all
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}\in A_{d}
, and the fact that
\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\alpha_{i}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}}\in\mathcal{A}
for all
m
and
i=1,2
, it is then straightforward to see that

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\alpha_{i}^{m}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}e^{\alpha_{i}^{m}(t-s)}\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}s.

(7.11)

This concludes the proof of Theorem
4.4
that
\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle
is solution to (
4.21
) and hence
Z_{t}=\left\langle\bm{\ell}^{\textnormal{DE}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle
by strong uniqueness.

8
Proof of Theorem
4.5

We first illustrate that
\left\langle\bm{\ell}_{t}^{\textnormal{GV}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle
with
\bm{\ell}_{t}^{\textnormal{GV}}
defined in (
4.33
) is a well-defined stochastic process.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left|K^{(n)}(t)\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}^{\otimes n}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\right|\mathrm{d}t\bigg]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left|K^{(n)}(t)\right|\mathbb{E}\left|\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}^{\otimes n}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\right|\mathrm{d}t

(8.1)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left|K^{(n)}(t)\right|\mathbb{E}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{s^{n}}{n!}dW_{s}\right|\mathrm{d}t

(8.2)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left|K^{(n)}(t)\right|\frac{t^{n+\frac{1}{2}}}{n!\sqrt{2n+1}}~\mathrm{d}t

(8.3)

The last inequality is derived from Jensen inequality. Then by assumption we know that

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left|K^{(n)}(t)\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}^{\otimes n}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\right|\mathrm{d}t\bigg]<\infty,

thus

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left|K^{(n)}(t)\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}^{\otimes n}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\right|<\infty,~\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\otimes\mathrm{d}t-a.e.

Consequently,

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}K^{(n)}(t)\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}^{\otimes n}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle
converges
\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\otimes\mathrm{d}t-a.e.
, ensuring
\left\langle\bm{\ell}_{t}^{\textnormal{GV}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle
to be a
\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\otimes\mathrm{d}t-a.e.
-well-defined map. Notice that for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\sum_{n=0}^{N}K^{(n)}(t)\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}^{\otimes n}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle
is progressively measurable, so
\left\langle\bm{\ell}_{t}^{\textnormal{GV}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle
is a also progressively measurable. It remains to prove that for every fixed
t
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{\ell}_{t}^{\textnormal{GV}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle=\int_{0}^{t}K(t-s)\mathrm{d}W_{s}.

(8.4)

For this we write

\displaystyle\left\langle\bm{\ell}_{t}^{\textnormal{GV}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}K^{(n)}(t)(-1)^{n}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}^{\otimes n}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}K^{(n)}(t)\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(-s)^{n}}{n!}\mathrm{d}W_{s}.

Since
\int_{0}^{t}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left|K^{(n)}(t)\right|\frac{s^{n}}{n!}\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}s<\infty
by assumption (
4.32
), the dominated convergence theorem of stochastic integrals yields

\sum_{n=0}^{N}K^{(n)}(t)\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(-s)^{n}}{n!}\mathrm{d}W_{s}\longrightarrow\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}K^{(n)}(t)\frac{(-s)^{n}}{n!}\mathrm{d}W_{s}=\int_{0}^{t}K(t-s)\mathrm{d}W_{s},

in probability, as
N\to\infty
. While the previous analysis ensures that

\sum_{n=0}^{N}K^{(n)}(t)\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(-s)^{n}}{n!}\mathrm{d}W_{s}\overset{\text{a.s.}}{\longrightarrow}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}K^{(n)}(t)\int_{0}^{t}\frac{(-s)^{n}}{n!}\mathrm{d}W_{s}=\left\langle\bm{\ell}_{t}^{\textnormal{GV}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle,

which proves (
8.4
) and completes the proof.

Appendix A
Properties of the resolvent

We start by deriving a transformation formula, which is simple but crucial to simplify the algebraic expressions.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\bm{\ell}\in T((\mathbb{R}^{d}))
,
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\in A_{d}
and
\bm{b}:=\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}\in A_{d}}\bm{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}
for
\bm{b}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}\in\mathbb{R}
, then

\bm{\ell}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{b}}=e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{b}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\left((e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-\bm{b}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\ell}){{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\right).

Proof.

Let
\bm{\gamma}:=\bm{\ell}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{b}}-e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{b}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\left(\left(e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-\bm{b}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\ell}\right){{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\right)
, then

\displaystyle\bm{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\bm{\ell}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\left({\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{b}}\bm{b}\right)-\left({\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{b}}\bm{b}\right)\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\left(\left(e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-\bm{b}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\ell}\right){{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\bm{\ell}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}+\bm{\ell}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{b}}\bm{b}-\left[{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\left(e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-\bm{b}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\ell}\right)\right]{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}-\left[(e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{b}}\bm{b})\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\left(e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-\bm{b}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\ell}\right)\right]{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}-\left[e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{b}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}{\left(e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-\bm{b}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\ell}\right){{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}}\right]\bm{b}

\displaystyle=\left[\bm{\ell}-\left(e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{b}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-\bm{b}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\ell}\right)\right]{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}+\left[\bm{\ell}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{b}}-e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{b}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\left(\left(e^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}-\bm{b}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\ell}\right){{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\right)\right]\bm{b}

\displaystyle=0+\bm{\gamma}\bm{b},

implying that
\bm{\gamma}=0
.
∎

We then provide the useful decomposition of the unique solution of the linear equation
\bm{\ell}=\bm{p}+\bm{\ell}\bm{q}
.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
\bm{p},\bm{q}\in T((\mathbb{R}^{d}))
such that
\bm{q}^{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}\neq 1
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\in A_{d}
. Then

\left(\bm{p}{\left({\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}-\bm{q}\right)}^{-1}\right)|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}}=\frac{1}{1-\bm{q}^{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}}\left[\bm{p}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}}+\bm{p}{\left({\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}-\bm{q}\right)}^{-1}(\bm{q}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}})\right].

Proof.

First recall the decomposition in Remark
2.1
, i.e.

\bm{q}=\bm{q}^{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\in A_{d}}\bm{q}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}},

and Proposition
2.2
, i.e.

{\left({\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}-\bm{q}\right)}^{-1}={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+{\left({\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}-\bm{q}\right)}^{-1}\bm{q}.

One can then easily get that

\displaystyle{\left({\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}-\bm{q}\right)}^{-1}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+{\left({\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}-\bm{q}\right)}^{-1}\left(\bm{q}^{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\in A_{d}}\bm{q}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\right)=\frac{1}{1-\bm{q}^{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}}\left({\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}+{\left({\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}-\bm{q}\right)}^{-1}\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\in A_{d}}\bm{q}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\right).

(A.1)

It then follows that

\displaystyle\bm{p}{\left({\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}-\bm{q}\right)}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\bm{q}^{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}}\left(\bm{p}+\bm{p}{\left({\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}-\bm{q}\right)}^{-1}\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\in A_{d}}\bm{q}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{i}}}\right).

(A.2)

Taking the projection ends the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Proof of Proposition
3.5

In this section, we are specifically interested in the case
X_{t}=\widehat{W}_{t}=(t,W_{t}^{2},\dots,W_{t}^{d})
where
W=(W^{2},\dots,W^{d})
is a (
d-1
)-dimensional Brownian motion. We will denote
n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})
the size of
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}
, i.e. its number of letters, and
x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})
the number of
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}
s specifically. If it does not cause ambiguity, we will write
n
for
n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})
and
x
for
x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})
.

Let
g^{p}:[0,T]\times V\to\mathbb{R}
be defined for all even
p\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
by

\displaystyle g_{t}^{p}({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})=\left[\prod_{j=2}^{p}\binom{jn}{n}\right]\frac{t^{\frac{p}{2}(n+x)}}{\left(\frac{p}{2}(n+x)\right)!}2^{\frac{p}{2}(x-n)}

(B.1)

and for all odd
p\in\mathbb{N}
by

\displaystyle g_{t}^{p}({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})=\left(g_{t}^{p+1}({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})\right)^{\frac{p}{p+1}},

(B.2)

for all
t\in[0,T]
and all
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V
. Notice that
g_{t}^{p}({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})
only depends on
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}
through its size and the number of its
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}
s, i.e.
n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})
and
x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})
.

Proposition B.1
.

Fix
t\in[0,T]
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\right|^{p}\right]\leq g_{t}^{p}({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}),\quad{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V,\quad p\in\mathbb{N}.

(B.3)

Proof.

We first use Fawcett’s formula
[
37
, Proposition 4.10]
to write

\mathbb{E}\left[\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right]=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{t^{m}}{m!}\left({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}\in\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}},\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{d}}}\right\}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{jj}}}\right)^{\otimes m}.

We then define for all
n\in\mathbb{N}

\widehat{V}_{n}:=\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V_{n}:\exists j\in\mathbb{N}\text{ such that }{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}={{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u_{1}\cdots u_{j}}}}\text{ for some }{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u_{k}}}}\in\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{22}}},\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{dd}}}\right\}\text{ with }k=1,2,\dots,j\right\}.

(B.4)

This set corresponds to the coordinate of every signature element with non-zero expectation, i.e. for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V_{n}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\right]=\begin{cases}\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}(n+x)}}{\left(\frac{1}{2}(n+x)\right)!}\cdot 2^{\frac{1}{2}(x-n)}&\text{if }{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in\widehat{V}_{n},\\
0&\text{else}.\end{cases}

(B.5)

We are now ready to compute even moments of every signature element. First, remark that for all
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\in V_{k}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V_{m}
,
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}
is a sum of
\binom{k+m}{k}
elements
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}\in V_{k+m}
of length
n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}})=k+m
each, having exactly
x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}})=x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})
number of
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}
s, so that

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\right]\leq\binom{k+m}{k}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}\in V_{k+m}\\
\textnormal{s.t. }x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}})=x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle.

Consequently, for every
p\in\mathbb{N}
, we can iteratively apply the shuffle product
p
-times, and obtain the claimed upper bound for the even powers of the signature:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle^{2p}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}^{\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}2p},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\leq\left[\prod_{j=2}^{2p}\binom{jn}{n}\right]\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}\in V_{2pn}\\
s.t.\>x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}})=2p\>x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle=\left[\prod_{j=2}^{2p}\binom{jn}{n}\right]\frac{t^{p(n+x)}}{(p(n+x))!}2^{p(x-n)}.

(B.6)

We can finally treat the odd moments by applying Jensen’s inequality on the convex function
y\to y^{\frac{2p}{2p-1}}
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle\right|^{2p-1}\right]^{\frac{1}{2p-1}}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{t}\right\rangle^{2p}\right]^{\frac{1}{2p}}\leq\left(h_{t}^{2p}({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})\right)^{\frac{1}{2p}}=\left(h_{t}^{2p-1}({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})\right)^{\frac{1}{2p-1}},

(B.7)

concluding the proof of the proposition.
∎

We are now ready to prove Proposition
3.5
:

Proof of Proposition
3.5
.

Proposition
3.5
-
(i)
follows directly from an application of the above Proposition
B.1
. In order to prove (
3.16
) we will consider two cases: either when word
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}
ends with a
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}}
or it ends with a
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}
in
\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}},\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{d}}}\right\}
.

(1)

Case
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}={{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u1}}}
for some word
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\in V
.

We apply Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left(\int_{0}^{s}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{u}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}u\right)^{2}\right]

(B.8)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{t}\left|\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{u}\right\rangle\right|\mathrm{d}u\right)^{2}\right]

(B.9)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[t\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{u}\right\rangle^{2}\mathrm{d}u\right]

(B.10)

\displaystyle=t\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{u}\right\rangle^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}u.

(B.11)

Thus, using (
3.15
) with
n=n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})=n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+1
and
x=x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})=x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\binom{2(n-1)}{n-1}\frac{t}{2^{n-x}(n+x-2)!}\int_{0}^{t}u^{n+x-2}\mathrm{d}u

(B.12)

\displaystyle=\binom{2(n-1)}{n-1}\frac{t^{n+x}}{2^{n-x}(n+x-1)!}

(B.13)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{n+x}}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{t^{n+x}}{(n+x-1)!},

(B.14)

where the last inequality comes from an application of Stirling’s approximation that reads
\binom{2m}{m}\leq\frac{2^{2m}}{\sqrt{m+1}}
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

(2)

Case
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}={{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{uj}}}
for some word
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\in V
and some letter
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}\in\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{2}}},\dots,{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{d}}}\right\}
.
First we apply Theorem
3.3
to get

\displaystyle\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{0}^{s}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{u}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}W_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{s}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{u}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}u\right)^{2}

(B.15)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\int_{0}^{s}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{u}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}W_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\int_{0}^{s}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{u}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}u\right)^{2}.

(B.16)

Now, since
\int_{0}^{s}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{u}\right\rangle dW_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}
is a true martingale, we apply Doob’s inequality followed by the previous result (
B.14
) with
n=n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})=n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+1
and
x=x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})=x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})
and get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left(\int_{0}^{s}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{u}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}W_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}\right)^{2}\right]\leq 4~\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{u}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}W_{u}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}\right]^{2}=4\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{u}\right\rangle^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}u\leq 4\frac{2^{n+x}}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{t^{n+x}}{(n+x)!}.

(B.17)

Then, again applying (
B.14
) to the right-most element in (
B.16
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left(\int_{0}^{s}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{u}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}u\right)^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{1}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle^{2}\right]\leq\frac{2^{n+x}}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{t^{n+x}}{(n+x-1)!}.

(B.18)

Note that if
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}|_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{j}}}}=0
, the left-hand side is 0 and the equation still holds.

Combining the above inequalities, we derive

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\frac{2^{n+x-1}}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{t^{n+x}}{(n+x-1)!},

(B.19)

for some
C_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
independent of
t
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}
.

Combining the two equalities (
B.14
)-(
B.19
) above, we have that for every
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V_{n}
,
n\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left|\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle\right|^{2}\right]\leq C_{2}\frac{2^{n+x-1}}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{t^{n+x}}{(n+x-1)!}.

(B.20)

for some
C_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
independent of
t
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}
.

Moreover, as
n+1\sim n
, there exists a constant
C
such that

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left|\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},\widehat{\mathbb{W}}_{s}\right\rangle\right|^{2}\right]\leq C\frac{2^{n+x-1}}{\sqrt{n+1}}\frac{t^{n+x}}{(n+x-1)!}.

(B.21)

This includes the case
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}\in V_{0}
and proves (
3.16
).

It remains to prove the sub-multiplicative property (
3.17
).
First, in the case
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}
or
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}={\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}
, (
3.17
) holds trivially. Now we can easily see that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{h_{t}({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})h_{t}({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})}{h_{t}({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=C^{2}A({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})B({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}),

(B.22)

where

\displaystyle A({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}):=\frac{(n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})-1)!}{(n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})-1)!(n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})-1)!}\quad\text{and}\quad B({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}):=\sqrt{\frac{n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})+1}{(n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+1)(n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})+1)}}.

(B.23)

We now only need to show that
C^{2}A({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})B({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})\geq 1
for
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}
. Without loss of generality, we assume
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\textup{{\o{}}}}
, i.e.
n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})>0
, otherwise we could simply swap
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}
in the following equations. Let us then remark that

\displaystyle A({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})

\displaystyle=\binom{n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})-1}{n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})}(n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}))

(B.24)

and that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+1}

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}(n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}))

(B.25)

\displaystyle\sqrt{n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})+1}\sqrt{n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+1}

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}\sqrt{n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})+1}(n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}))\leq C^{2}\sqrt{n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})+1}(n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})),

(B.26)

which implies

\displaystyle B({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})\geq\frac{1}{C^{2}(n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}))}

(B.27)

and thus

\displaystyle\left(\frac{h_{t}({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})h_{t}({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})}{h_{t}({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\binom{n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}))+n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}})-1}{n({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})+x({{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}})}\geq 1.

(B.28)

∎

Appendix C
Proof of Proposition
3.1

Let
n\leq k\in\mathbb{N}
, then for all
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\in V_{n}
,
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V_{k-n}
, each element of
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}
is of size
k
. This leads to the fact that
\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}\right\rangle=0
whenever
{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}\notin V_{k}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},\mathbb{X}_{t}\right\rangle=\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}\in V_{k}}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}\right\rangle\mathbb{X}_{t}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}.

(C.1)

Moreover, by bilinearity of the shuffle product and for
\bm{\ell},\bm{\varphi}\in T((\mathbb{R}^{d}))
,

\displaystyle\langle\bm{\ell}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\varphi},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\in V_{n}}\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V_{m}}\bm{\ell}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\bm{\varphi}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}\rangle=\sum_{n=0}^{k}\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\in V_{n}}\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V_{k-n}}\bm{\ell}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\bm{\varphi}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}\rangle.

(C.2)

Hence, it is easy to see that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}\in V_{k}}\left\langle\bm{\ell}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\varphi},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}\right\rangle\mathbb{X}_{t}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}=\sum_{n=0}^{k}\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\in V_{n}}\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V_{k-n}}\bm{\ell}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\bm{\varphi}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},\mathbb{X}_{t}\right\rangle.

(C.3)

It then follows that the semi-norm of
\bm{\ell}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\varphi}
is of the form

\displaystyle\left|\left|\bm{\ell}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\varphi}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}\in V_{k}}\left\langle\bm{\ell}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\varphi},{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}\right\rangle\mathbb{X}_{t}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{w}}}\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\sum_{n=0}^{k}\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\in V_{n}}\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V_{k-n}}\bm{\ell}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\bm{\varphi}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},\mathbb{X}_{t}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n=0}^{k}\left|\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\in V_{n}}\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V_{k-n}}\bm{\ell}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\bm{\varphi}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\left\langle{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}},\mathbb{X}_{t}\right\rangle\right|.

Finally, using the shuffle product property on the right-hand side, recall Lemma
2.5
, the semi-norm can be bounded

\displaystyle\left|\left|\bm{\ell}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\varphi}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n=0}^{k}\left|\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\in V_{n}}\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V_{k-n}}\bm{\ell}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\bm{\varphi}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\mathbb{X}_{t}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\mathbb{X}_{t}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\right|\leq\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left|\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\in V_{n}}\bm{\ell}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\mathbb{X}_{t}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{u}}}\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\in V_{m}}\bm{\varphi}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\mathbb{X}_{t}^{{\color[rgb]{0.06,0.46,1}\mathbf{v}}}\right|\leq\left|\left|\bm{\ell}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\left|\left|\bm{\varphi}\right|\right|_{t}^{\mathcal{A}}<\infty.

It can then be argued very similarly that
\left\langle\bm{\ell},\mathbb{X}_{t}\right\rangle\left\langle\bm{\varphi},\mathbb{X}_{t}\right\rangle=\left\langle\bm{\ell}\mathrel{\sqcup\mkern-3.0mu\sqcup}\bm{\varphi},\mathbb{X}_{t}\right\rangle
, ending the proof.
